As Feb 29, 2020 (data cut-off date), a total of 6 subjects with platinum-sensitive, BRCA mutationpositive ovarian cancer were enrolled, all of whom had different degrees of tumour shrinkage, with a disease control rate of 63.4% and a tumour response rate of 22%. Although no clear correlation between dose and efficacy was observed, pharmacokinetic data showed a positive correlation trend between drug exposure and efficacy endpoints and treatment-related AEs, while plasma concentrations showed a saturation trend in the 80 to100 mg groups. The sponsor and the investigator decided to take 100 mg QD as recommended dosing regimen for the phase III study, upon taking into account factors such as efficacy, safety and pharmacokinetic profile.

## 5.3.2. Main study

## 5.3.2.1. IMP4297-301 FLAMES

## 5.3.2.1.1. Study title

A Phase III Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Multicenter Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Senaparib Maintenance Monotherapy Following First-Line Platinum-based Chemotherapy in Subjects with FIGO Stage III-IV Ovarian Cancer.

## 5.3.2.1.2. Study design

## Figure 20: Study schema of study

<!-- image -->

Stratification factors:

-  Response to first-line platinum-based chemotherapy: complete response/partial response
-  Baseline BRCA status: BRCA mutation/BRCA wild-type

RECIST v1.1 assessment schedule: every 12 weeks ±7 days for the first 120 weeks, then every 24 weeks ±7 days thereafter Abbreviations: BRCA = breast cancer susceptibility gene; CR = complete remission; FIGO = International Federation of Gynaecology and Obstetrics; N = number of subjects in the analysis population; PFS = progression-free survival; PO = oral administration; PR = partial remission; QD = once daily; RECIST = Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumours.

Regardless of whether they continue receiving study treatment, all subjects should continue to undergo tumour assessments until progressive disease (PD) or the subject begins receiving other antitumour therapy. Once the subject discontinues study treatment, the investigator decides whether they will receive other therapy.

The study was only conducted in China, and first informed consent form was signed in November 2019, when no PARP inhibitor was approved for the first line maintenance therapy of ovarian cancer regardless of BRCA status. Therefore, the placebo was considered as the control arm.

Tumour assessments were performed at baseline, every 12 weeks (± 1 week) from the date of randomization until 120 weeks, then every 24 weeks (± 1 week) until objective imaging PD according to Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumours (RECIST) v1.1. All computed tomography (CT)/magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan data were sent to a third-party Clinical Research Organization designated by the sponsor for blinded independent central review (BICR) review. After the primary efficacy analysis, BICR assessments were no longer performed.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Treatment

Subjects enrolled in the study received senaparib or matching placebo at the dose of 100 mg once daily (QD) orally on an empty stomach, in 28-day cycles, until the occurrence of any of the following: objective imaging progression, subject decision to end treatment, intolerable AEs, pregnancy, severe noncompliance with protocol and/continuous interruption of the investigational drug for more than 28 days due to non-adverse events, or having received the planned dose for 2 years.

Subjects with evidence of stable disease (i.e. no evidence of PD) at 2 years may continue to receive investigational drug if the investigator assesses that the benefits outweigh the risks of continuing investigational drug from a benefit-risk perspective. However, if subjects have no evidence of disease at 2 years, the study treatment should be discontinued. Subjects may need to discontinue treatment at any time if deemed inappropriate by the investigator in the interest of the subjects.

## Randomisation

Subjects successfully enrolled were randomly assigned at a 2:1 ratio to either senaparib or placebo.

Randomization was stratified according to the following criteria: Response to first-line platinum-based chemotherapy (CR/PR), Baseline BRCA status (BRCA mutant / BRCA wild type).

## Blinding

This was a double-blind trial. From randomization to database lock, subjects, investigators, study coordinators, or the sponsor's team and its authorised representatives were kept blinded to each subject's treatment or control group assignment.

## Patient population

## Main inclusion criteria included:

1. *Subjects have the ability to read and understand, communicate effectively with the investigator and understand and sign the Informed Consent Form (ICF) in writing. If the subject or guardian does not have the ability to read and understand, an impartial witness shall be present to witness the whole process of informed consent. Subjects have to provide ICF prior to any study-specified procedures;
2. *Subjects must be female ≥18 years of age;
3. Newly diagnosed, histologically confirmed FIGO stage III-IV high grade serous, high grade endometrioid or other histological types of epithelial ovarian cancer, fallopian tube cancer or primary peritoneal cancer carrying BRCA mutation.
4. Stage III subjects should have had one prior optimal cytoreductive surgery (primary or interval cytoreductive surgery). Stage IV subjects must have had a biopsy and/or primary cytoreductive surgery or interval cytoreductive surgery;
5. Subjects who have completed first-line platinum-based (e.g., carboplatin or cisplatin) chemotherapy (intravenous or intraperitoneal) prior to randomization:
- Must have CR or PR after completion of this chemotherapy at the discretion of the investigator. CR is defined as absence of measurable and/or non-

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

measurable disease per RECIST v1.1 by imaging assessment and with CA125 of the normal range. PR is defined as achievement of PR per RECIST v1.1 by imaging assessment, or absence of measurable and/or non-measurable lesion per RECIST v1.1 by imaging assessment following chemotherapy, with CA125 higher than the normal range and should not continuously increased;

- First-line platinum-based chemotherapy must consist of a minimum of 6 to a maximum of 9 platinum chemotherapy cycles. Subjects who have prematurely discontinued platinum chemotherapy due to toxicity from the platinum chemotherapy regimen must have received at least 4 cycles of platinum-based chemotherapy;
- *Subjects can't use vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF)/vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) inhibitors and/or immune checkpoint inhibitors in combination or maintenance treatment during the course of first-line therapy;
- Subjects can't receive any investigational drug during the first-line chemotherapy;
- Subjects must be randomised within 8 weeks after the last dose of the chemotherapy (the last dose is the day of the last infusion);
6. The test results of CA125 before treatment must meet the following specific criteria:
- If the first test value is ≤upper limit of normal (ULN), the subject can be randomised without second sampling;
- If the first test value is &gt;ULN, a second test must be performed at least 7 days after the first. If the value of the subject's second test is ≥15% higher than that of the first, the subject is not eligible for inclusion;
7. Subjects must have normal organ and bone marrow function, as indicated by the latest examination within 28 days prior to first dose of investigational drug (blood products, including but not limited to blood transfusion or biological products (with biological products including erythropoietin, recombinant thrombopoietin, granulocyte colony-stimulating factor and granulocyte macrophage colony-stimulating factor, etc.) within ≤ 28 days prior to first dose of investigational drug are not allowed).
8. Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) Performance Status score 0-1
9. *Postmenopausal women or women with childbearing potential who have proved not pregnant.

* should be met before the subject is submitted for BRCA testing,

## Main exclusion criteria included:

1. BRCA mutation status is unclear;
2. *Subjects with early disease (FIGO stage I or II);
3. Subjects with tumour assessment of stable disease (SD) or PD after first-line chemotherapy according to RECIST v1.1 by imaging assessment;
4. *More than one cytoreductive surgery for progressive disease prior to study randomisation. (Subjects with tumour considered unresectable at diagnosis and with biopsy or oophorectomy only, followed by continued chemotherapy and interval cytoreductive surgery; or subjects who

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

have not achieved satisfactory cytoreductive effect following primary cytoreductive surgery and are willing to undergo supplementary cytoreductive surgery can be enrolled in the study);

5. *Subjects with previously received chemotherapy for any abdominal or pelvic tumour, including treatment of previously diagnosed early stage ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer;
6. Subjects with ascites drawn during the last two chemotherapy cycles prior to study enrolment;
7. Subjects who have received strong CYP3A4 inhibitors or strong CYP3A4 inducers before the first dose of the investigational drug (≥ 5 half-lives of washout period from the first dose of the investigational drug can be enrolled) and need to continue to receive these drugs during the study;
8. *Have received drugs targeting poly-ADP-ribose polymerase (PARP).

* should not be met before the subject is submitted for BRCA testing, and all exclusion criteria should not be met before randomisation

## 5.3.2.1.3. Objectives and estimands

## Primary objective

The primary objective of the trial was to demonstrate the superior efficacy of senaparib as a maintenance treatment compared with placebo, based on progression free survival (PFS) as assessed by BICR based on RECIST v1.1, in patients with FIGO stage III-IV ovarian cancer following first-line platinum-based chemotherapy.

## Estimands for the primary objective

Table 23: Estimands for primary objective

| Population                                          | Patients with FIGO Stage III-IV Ovarian Cancer Following First-line Platinum-based Chemotherapy                                                                                                                              |
|-----------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Treatment condition<s>                              | Assignment to senaparib in the hypothetical scenario of no subsequent cancer therapy before progression, compared to assignment to placebo in the hypothetical scenario of no subsequent initiation of anti-tumour treatment |
| Endpoint (variable)                                 | Progression Free Survival (PFS) defined as the time from date of randomisation to date of PD (assessed by BICR according to RECIST v1.1) or death from any cause, whichever occurs first                                     |
| Population-level summary                            | Hazard Ratio (HR) from a Cox proportional hazards model stratified by randomisation stratification factors.                                                                                                                  |
| Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them     | Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Start of new anti- tumour treatment prior to PD     | Hypothetical: censored in primary analysis at the date of last imaging examination prior to initiation of new anti-tumour treatment, thereby ignoring data after new anti-tumour treatment and assuming censored at random.  |
| Two or more missing imaging assessments prior to PD | Hypothetical: censored in primary analysis at the date of last imaging examination prior to two or more missing assessments, thereby ignoring data after two or more missing assessments.                                    |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

| Death   | Composite: event, to reflect treatment failure   |
|---------|--------------------------------------------------|

The primary objective is a comparison of PFS between senaparib and placebo arms, in the hypothetical scenario no subsequent cancer therapy before progression. The intercurrent events (ICE) of initiation of new anti-tumour treatment before progression is handled using a hypothetical strategy, by censoring patients at the date of last imaging examination before its occurrence. In other words, data following new anti-tumour treatment are ignored and the corresponding patients are assumed to be censored at random (CAR).

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimands

## Analysis populations

Intention-to-treat analyses (ITT) population: includes all randomised subjects. Subjects are analysed according to their randomised treatment assignment, regardless of the treatment actually received. Baseline characteristics, efficacy, and health-related quality of life are analysed based on the ITT population.

Per protocol set (PPS) population: includes subjects in the ITT population who have no major protocol deviations affecting efficacy assessments. Analyses are performed by randomised treatment. This population is used for sensitivity analysis of efficacy.

## Analysis of the primary endpoint

The primary efficacy endpoint of this study is PFS as assessed by BICR based on RECIST v1.1. Median PFS, PFS rate at different time points and its 95% CI were estimated using Kaplan-Meier method, and survival curve was plotted. Comparison of PFS between groups was performed using a stratified logrank test. A stratified Cox proportional hazards model was used to estimate the hazard ratio (HR) and its 95% CI. Stratification factors at randomization were used, including response to first-line platinumbased chemotherapy (CR and PR) as well as BRCA mutation status at baseline (BRCAmut and BRCAwt).

The PFS primary set of censoring rules is summarised in the table below.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Table 24 PFS primary set of censoring rules

| Situation                                                                                                                                              | Censoring Rules for Primary Analysis - Censoring/Event   | Censoring Rules for Primary Analysis - Time point                                 |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| No baseline or post- baseline imaging examinations, and no death occurs during the first two scheduled post- randomization imaging examination periods | Censored                                                 | Date of randomization                                                             |
| No baseline or post- baseline imaging examinations, and death occurs during the first two scheduled post- randomization imaging examination periods    | Event                                                    | Date of death                                                                     |
| No PD or death occurs, with no new anti-tumor treatment initiated                                                                                      | Censored                                                 | Date of last imaging examination                                                  |
| No PD, start of new anti-tumor treatment                                                                                                               | Censored                                                 | Date of last imaging examination prior to initiation of new anti- tumor treatment |
| No occurrence of two or more missed imaging examinations prior to PD or death                                                                          | Event                                                    | Date of PD or death                                                               |
| PD or death after two or more consecutive missed imaging examinations                                                                                  | Censored                                                 | Date of last imaging examination prior to PD or death                             |

## Sensitivity analyses

The primary efficacy analysis was repeated replacing the BICR-assessed PFS with investigatorassessed PFS.

The primary efficacy analysis was repeated replacing the stratification factors recorded at randomisation with the actual stratification factors.

The primary efficacy analysis was repeated replacing the ITT population with the PPS population.

A tipping point analysis of the PFS analysis was also performed, exploring the potential impact of non administrative censoring on the PFS results.

Supplementary analyses based on alternative estimands

The primary efficacy analysis was repeated with the estimand for PFS modified for the following intercurrent events:

- No PD, start of new anti-tumour treatment
- PD or death after two or more consecutive missed imaging examination

The former used a composite strategy, in which the initiation of new anti-tumour treatment was considered a treatment failure, i.e. a PFS event and the event date was the date of the new antitumour therapy; the latter used a treatment policy strategy, in which PD or death after the intercurrent event was considered a PFS event and the event date was the date of PD or death.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A post-hoc additional analysis was performed in the clinical study report addendum, based on a supplementary estimand. Based on this supplementary estimand, the ICEs start of new anti-tumour treatment and two or more missing imaging assessments before PD are handled using a treatment policy strategy (no censoring applied in both situations).

## Sample size determination

A total of 393 subjects were planned to be randomised in a 2:1 ratio to senaparib or placebo, corresponding to 262 subjects in the senaparib group and 131 in the placebo group.

The sample size was calculated using a two-sided significance level of 0.05 and 90% power, assuming a median PFS of 13.8 months in the placebo group and 21.56 months in the senaparib group, corresponding to a hazard ratio of 0.64. A dropout rate of 5% per year was assumed in each group. Enrolment was planned to last 20 months, with the final PFS analysis to be performed after approximately 234 PFS events, expected at month 38. An interim PFS efficacy analysis was planned after approximately 164 events, representing 70% of the total planned PFS events, using the LanDeMets method with an O'Brien-Fleming boundary.

The final overall survival (OS) analysis was planned after 160 deaths. Assuming a median OS of 80 months in the senaparib group and 64 months in the placebo group, this number of events was expected to provide approximately 26% power to detect a hazard ratio of 0.8.

## Multiplicity adjustment

The analysis of OS was included in the overall type I error control using hierarchical testing, i.e., further formal statistical testing of OS was only to be performed if the PFS analysis was significant.

## Interim analyses

An interim analysis of PFS efficacy was planned at approximately 164 PFS events (approximately 70% of the total number of PFS events) to determine whether the efficacy boundary was met for PFS. Using the Lan-Demets method with O'Brien Fleming boundary, α was set to 0.015 for the interim analysis and 0.046 for the final analysis.

As of the data cutoff date for this interim analysis report (March 16, 2023), 170 PFS events had occurred, which met the preset conditions for conducting an interim analysis (approximately 164 PFS events). According to the protocol and SAP, the α value was adjusted from 0.015 to 0.0170.

The PFS efficacy interim analysis was performed by an independent third party. The Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC) reviewed the unblinded PFS efficacy interim analysis results and put forward corresponding recommendations.

Two interim analyses were planned for OS, which were to be conducted when 20% of the events (approximately 32 death events at PFS interim analysis) and 62.4% of the events (approximately 100 death events) occurred. Using the Lan-Demets method with O'Brien Fleming boundary, the corresponding critical values of type I error for three OS analyses are 1.08×10-6, 0.0092 and 0.047, respectively. The final critical value will be adjusted according to the actual number of events observed. The final analysis of OS is expected to occur approximately 80 months after the start of study.

## Secondary objectives

## Secondary objectives were:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- To assess other clinical benefits of senaparib maintenance monotherapy versus placebo:

To compare the effect on Health-related Quality of Life (HRQoL) of senaparib maintenance monotherapy versus placebo.

The secondary endpoint of OS uses the same test hypothesis as for PFS.

The analysis of OS was included in the overall type I error control using hierarchical testing. Hypothesis tests for other secondary endpoints were of exploratory nature.

Other secondary endpoints included PFS assessed by investigators according to RECIST v1.1, chemotherapy free interval (CFI), progression-free survival 2 (PFS2), time from randomization to first subsequent therapy or death (TFST), time from randomization to study treatment discontinuation or death (TDT) and the trial outcome index (TOI) of Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy -Ovarian (FACT-O).

## Estimands for the secondary objectives

Table 25: Estimands for secondary objective

| Population                                      | Patients with FIGO Stage III-IV Ovarian Cancer Following First-line Platinum-based Chemotherapy             |
|-------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Treatment condition<s>                          | Assignment to senaparib compared to assignment to placebo                                                   |
| Endpoint (variable)                             | Overall Survival (OS) defined as the time from date of randomisation to date of death from any cause        |
| Population-level summary                        | Hazard Ratio (HR) from a Cox proportional hazards model stratified by randomisation stratification factors. |
| Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them | Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them                                                             |
| Any intercurrent event (undefined)              | Treatment policy                                                                                            |

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on the secondary estimands

## PFS and PFS2 assessed by investigators, CFI, TFST, TDT and OS

PFS assessed by investigators, chemotherapy free interval (CFI), progression-free survival 2 (PFS2), time from randomization to first subsequent therapy or death (TFST), time from randomization to study treatment discontinuation or death (TDT) and overall survival (OS) were analysed using the same method for primary efficacy analysis.

## Using trial outcome index (TOI) of FACT-O to determine Proportion of improvement and Time to deterioration

The improved, worsened, and unchanged proportions of TOI were summarized and the proportion of improvement in TOI was compared between groups using logistic regression with response to first-line platinum-based chemotherapy at randomization and BRCA mutation status at baseline as covariates. Time to worsening of TOI was analysed in the same manner as the primary efficacy analysis.

For analysis populations, multiplicity control and interim analyses, please refer to the statistical methods for estimation on the primary estimand.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.3.2.1.4. Results

## 5.3.2.1.4.1. Participant flow and numbers analysed

This study was conducted in 41 sites across China from November 27, 2019 (date the first subject signed ICF) to March 16, 2023 (data cutoff date for the interim analysis report)

<!-- image -->

404 subjects were actually randomised, of which 403 subjects were treated according to the randomisation results. The main reason for the difference was due to rapid enrolment. When 393 subjects were determined eligible according to inclusion criteria, some subjects had signed the main ICF. Therefore, 10 more subjects were actually randomised than planned.

From November 27, 2019 (the date the first subject signed the ICF) to March 16, 2023 (data cutoff date), 668 subjects were screened and 34 subjects were re-screened and assigned to a new subject ID, therefore the total number of subject ID was 702.

During pre-screening, a total of 114 subjects failed, and the 3 main reasons of screening failure are as below:

- 35 patients did not meet inclusion criteria #3: Newly diagnosed, histologically confirmed FIGO stage III-IV HGS, HGE or other histological types of epithelial ovarian cancer, fallopian tube cancer or primary peritoneal cancer carrying BRCA mutation
- 21 patients did not meet inclusion criteria #11: Subjects are willing and able to comply with the study protocol during the study, regularly follow-up and examination
- 15 patients did not meet inclusion criteria #5: Subjects who have completed protocol required first-line platinum-based chemotherapy and achieved CR/PR

and 22 patients withdrew due to the subject's decisions during pre-screening.

During the screening period, a total of 150 subjects failed, and the 3 main reasons of screening failure are as below:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- 49 patients did not meet inclusion criteria #5: Subjects who have completed protocol required first-line platinum-based chemotherapy and achieved CR/PR
- 38 patients did not meet inclusion criteria #7: Subjects must have normal organ and bone marrow function
- 19 patients met exclusion criteria #15: Subjects with Myelodysplastic Syndrome (MDS)/Acute Myeloid Leukaemia (AML) and 3 patients withdrew due to the subject's decisions during screening.

In summary, the main reason for both pre-screen and screen failure is that the subjects do not meet the target population criteria. For example, some subjects either do not meet the criteria for FIGO stage III/IV advanced ovarian cancer or have not achieved CR/PR after platinum-based chemotherapy&gt;

## 5.3.2.1.4.2. Deviations from study plan

The protocol of study 301 was amended once, 2 years after the start of the study, notably to include patients with high grade endometrioid carcinoma since they can benefit from PARP inhibitors. In addition, an exploratory objective to assess the relationship between HRD and efficacy was also introduced. The latter implies that only patients after the implementation of this amendment were assessed for HRD status.

## Prior/Concomitant Therapy

## Prior Medications

Of the 404 subjects included in the ITT Population, 124 (30.7%) subjects reported the use of at least one prior medication (excluding anti-tumour medications).

In the senaparib group and the placebo group, 87/271 (32.1%) and 37/133 (27.8%) subjects, respectively, used at least one prior medication. By ATC2 classification, prior medications with use ≥5% in either Senaparib group or placebo group included immunoenhancers (11.8% vs. 14.3%), antianaemic agents (7.0% vs. 6.0%), unspecified herbal and traditional medicine (5.2% vs. 4.5%). By PT, prior medications with use ≥5% in either senaparib group or placebo group included garden burnet root (5.9% vs. 3.8%), and leucogen (4.1% vs. 6.8%).

The main types and use rates of prior medications were similar in the senaparib group and the placebo group.

## Concomitant Medications

Of the 404 subjects included in the ITT Population, 375 (92.8%) subjects reported the use of at least one concomitant medication.

In the senaparib group and the placebo group, 256/271 (94.5%) and 119/133 (89.5%) subjects, respectively, used at least one concomitant medication. By ATC2, concomitant medications with use 20% in either senaparib group or placebo group included immunoenhancers (70.5% vs. 30.8%), antianaemic agents (59.4% vs. 9.0%), haemostatic agents (53.9% vs. 8.3%), unspecified herbal and traditional medicine (45.0% vs. 33.1%), systemic antibacterials (37.6% vs. 27.8%), blood substitutes and perfusion solutions (25.5% vs. 5.3%), analgesics (24.7% vs. 17.3%), drugs for hepatobiliary disorders (24.4% vs. 18.0%), vitamins (24.4% vs. 14.3%), anti-inflammatory and anti-rheumatic products (21.0% vs. 10.5%). By PT, concomitant medications with use ≥20% in either senaparib group or placebo group included granulocyte colony stimulating factor (41.3% vs. 9.0%), garden burnet root (35.1% vs. 12.0%), oprelvekin (28.8% vs. 0), leucogen (26.6% vs. 13.5%), Shengxuebao Mixture 1 (28.0% vs. 2.3%), polysaccharide-iron complex (25.1% vs. 3.0%), recombinant human thrombopoietin (24.4% vs. 0.8%), Shengxuexiaoban Capsules 2 (21.4% vs. 2.3%). Compared with the placebo group, the use of concomitant medications was more common in the senaparib group (≥ 0%), which was associated with a higher incidence of common AEs in the senaparib group.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.3.2.1.4.4. Outcomes and estimation

As of March 16, 2023, 170 BICR PFS events had occurred, meeting the predefined condition for interim analysis (approximately 164 PFS events).

Efficacy results were summarised and analysed based on the ITT population (404 subjects). As of the data cutoff date, the median follow-up durations for the senaparib group and the placebo group were 22.37 months (0.1-38.2 months) and 22.18 months (4.2-35.4 months), respectively.

As of the DCO (Data Cut-off) date of the interim OS analysis (17 May 2024), 294 subjects remained in the study, with 2 patients still on treatment.

## Secondary outcomes

## Investigator-Assessed PFS

Investigator-assessed PFS has been evaluated in the sensitivity analysis of the primary analysis. In addition, both the BICR and the investigators assessed whether the subjects had PD, and overall, the concordance rate between BICR and investigator assessments was 87.9%.

## Chemotherapy-Free Interval (CFI)

The median CFI in the senaparib group has not yet been reached. Based on current data, compared with the placebo group (median CFI of 16.690 months [95% CI: 13.897, 23.589]), the senaparib group showed benefit in CFI: The stratified Cox proportional hazards model estimated HR was 0.41 (95% CI: 0.30, 0.55), and the stratified log-rank test P-value was &lt;0.0001.

## Time from Randomization to Second Progression (PFS2)

According to the investigators' assessment, 51 out of 271 subjects (18.8%) in the senaparib group and 25 out of 133 subjects (18.8%) in the placebo group experienced PFS2 events, respectively. However, the median PFS2 of both groups has not yet been reached; therefore, this data is for reference only.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Although the proportion of subjects in the placebo group who subsequently received PARP inhibitors (16.5%) was higher than that in the senaparib group (7.4%), a favourable trend in PFS2 can still be observed in the senaparib group.

## Time to First Subsequent Anti-Cancer Therapy or Death (TFST)

The median TFST in the senaparib group has not yet been reached. Based on current data, compared with the placebo group (median TFST was 14.390 months [95% CI: 11.269, 17.117]), the senaparib group demonstrated benefit in TFST: The stratified Cox proportional hazards model estimated HR was 0.44 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.59), and the stratified log-rank test P-value was &lt;0.0001.

## Time from Randomization to Discontinuation of Study Treatment or Death (TDT)

Compared with the placebo group (median TDT was 11.433 months [95% CI: 8.674, 14.062]), the senaparib group showed benefit with a median TDT of 23.359 months [95% CI: 17.248, 23.984]: the estimated HR from the stratified Cox proportional hazards model was 0.68 (95% CI: 0.54, 0.86), and the P-value of the stratified log-rank test was 0.0003.

## Overall Survival (OS)

As of the primary analysis (cut of date:16 March 2023), the censoring rates in both the senaparib group and the placebo group were high, and the OS data were immature; the median OS was not reached in either the senaparib group or the placebo group, and the OS rates at 12 and 24 months were 98.13% and 96.98%, and 88.97% and 89.59%, respectively. In the senaparib group and the placebo group, there were 30/271 subjects (11.1%) and 13/133 subjects (9.8%) of subject deaths; excluding dropouts for various reasons, there were 234/271 subjects (86.3%) and 118/133 subjects (88.7%) of subjects still undergoing survival follow-up in the senaparib group and the placebo group, respectively.

At the DCO date (17 May 2024) of the current interim OS analysis, the median follow-up time for the ITT population was 33 months, with a total of 100 deaths (out of a total sample size of 404), and the maturity was 24.8%. The HR obtained using the stratified COX model as planned in the statistical analysis plan was 0.78 (0.52, 1.17), indicating a trend toward benefit for senaparib with more mature data, and the median OS for both the senaparib group and the placebo group was not reached.

## Subsequent Anti-Tumour Therapy

Of the 404 subjects included in the ITT population, 189 (46.8%) subjects reported the use of at least one subsequent anti-tumour therapy.

In the senaparib group and the placebo group, 101/271 (37.3%) and 88/133 (66.2%) subjects, respectively, used at least one subsequent anti-tumour therapy. The main types (top 3) of subsequent anti-tumour therapies in the senaparib group or the placebo group included chemotherapy (66.3% vs. 68.2%), other (52.5% vs. 54.5%), and targeted therapy (44.6% vs. 45.5%). By PT, the subsequent anti-tumour therapies with overall use ≥ 20% included carboplatin (53.5% vs. 55.7%), bevacizumab (46.5% vs. 45.5%), nab-paclitaxel (37.6% vs. 21.6%), and paclitaxel (27.7% vs. 43.2%).

The proportion of subjects who received at least one subsequent anti-tumour therapy was lower in the senaparib group compared with the placebo group.

## Patient-Reported Outcomes (PROs) Based on the Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy-Ovarian (FACT-O)

Overall, the proportions of subjects who completed visits as scheduled were high in both groups, and missing visits were balanced.

Based on the ITT population, for visits with the number of subjects ≥ 10 in each group, the change from baseline in the TOI value of FACT-O was assessed using MMRM. Overall, in the senaparib group and the placebo group, the least square means (standard error) of the change from baseline in the TOI value of FACT-O were 2.3996 (0.4835) (n = 266, P-value &lt; 0.0001) and 3.8693 (0.6801) (n = 133, P-value &lt; 0.0001), respectively; the inter-group comparison showed that least square mean difference (standard error) was -1.4697 (0.8343) (95% CI: -3.1106, 0.1712, P-value = 0.0790).

.

## BRCA Status

Based on the ITT population in Study 301, the impact of BRCA status on PFS assessed by BICR was analysed. The HRs for the subgroups with baseline BRCA mutation and BRCA wildtype status were 0.43 (95% CI: 0.24, 0.76) and 0.43 (95% CI: 0.30, 0.61), with P values of 0.0026 and &lt;0.0001, respectively, indicating that regardless of BRCA status, subjects can benefit or have a reduced risk of PD or death.

In addition, a similar subgroup analysis of PFS based on investigator assessments was conducted, showing that the HRs for the subgroups with BRCA status were 0.33 (95% CI: 0.20, 0.56) and 0.48 (95% CI: 0.34, 0.67), respectively, with P values both &lt;0.0001.

## Homologous Recombination Deficiency (HRD)

At the DCO date of the previously reported primary analysis of PFS (16 March 2023), tumour tissue samples were collected from 271 patients and were analysed retrospectively, out of which, samples from 222 patients were appropriate for obtaining HRD test data.

Among the 222 patients who underwent HRD testing, the median progression-free survival (PFS) was not reached in the senaparib group, while it was 16.6 months (11.6 - 21.4) in the placebo group, with a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.46 (95% CI 0.30 - 0.70).

Among HRD-positive patients (N=161), the median progression-free survival (PFS) was not reached in the senaparib group, while it was 15.7 months (11.0 - 21.3) in the placebo group, with a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.36 (95% CI 0.22 - 0.61).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Among HRP (Homologous Recombination Proficient) patients (N=61), the median PFS in the senaparib group was 27.1 months (8.4-NR), and in the placebo group it was 19.5 months (7.8-NR), with an HR of 0.74 (95% CI 0.36-1.54).

Among HRD-positive and BRCAwt patients (N=86), the median progression-free survival (PFS) was not reached in the senaparib group, while it was 12.9 months (8.3 - 21.3) in the placebo group, with a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.30 (95% CI 0.15 - 0.60).

Among patients with undetermined HRD status (N=182), the median PFS was NR in the senaparib group and 11.0 months (5.6 - 15.6) in the placebo group, with an HR of 0.43 (95% CI 0.27 - 0.67).

Based on the 222 patients who underwent HRD testing, the PFS KM curves by HRD status are shown in Figure 26, Figure 27 and Figure 28.

5.3.2.1.4.6. Pre-defined and post-hoc sensitivity analyses

Pre-defined sensitivity analysis

Investigator-Assessed PFS Based on RECIST v1.1: The median PFS in the senaparib group has not yet been reached, while median PFS in the placebo group was 11.105 months [95% CI: 9.396, 15.474]), with a HR of 0.43 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.57; P value &lt;0.0001).

BICR-Assessed PFS Based on RECIST v1.1 (Statistical Analysis Using Actual Stratification

Factors): Except for 6 subjects (1.49%) in the senaparib group and 1 subject (0.2%) in the placebo group whose stratification results at randomisation were inconsistent with their actual stratification results, all other subjects maintained consistency between randomisation stratification and actual stratification results. Statistical analysis conducted according to the actual stratification results showed that the median PFS in the senaparib group has not yet been reached. In the placebo group, median PFS was 13.634 months [95% CI: 10.875, 16.624]), and the HR was 0.43 (95% CI: 0.31, 0.58; P value &lt;0.0001).

BICR-Assessed PFS Based on RECIST v1.1 (PPS Population): Among the 400 subjects included in the PPS population, comprising 269 in the senaparib group and 131 in the placebo group, the median PFS in the senaparib group has not yet been reached. In the placebo group, median PFS was 13.634 months [95% CI: 10.875, 16.624]), with a HR of 0.43 (95% CI: 0.31, 0.58; P-value &lt; 0.0001).

BICR-Assessed PFS Based on RECIST v1.1 (Sensitivity Analysis Censoring Rules): Compared to the censoring rules of the primary analysis, the analysis using the sensitivity analysis censoring rules showed that, a median PFS of 10.973 months [95% CI: 8.345, 13.634] in the placebo group (), and a median PFS of 26.842 months [95% CI: 19.384, NA]) in the senaparib group, with a HR of 0.48 (95% CI: 0.37, 0.63; P-value &lt; 0.0001).

Post-hoc sensitivity analysis

Alternative censoring rule An alternative censoring rule that reflects the \"treatment policy\" strategy in the estimand framework is applied to the PFS analysis. Specifically, if a subject starts new anti-tumour therapy or misses any scheduled imaging assessments before PD, the imaging assessments (if any) or death afterwards will be followed and included in the analysis, i.e. following the treatment policy strategy.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The alternative censoring rule is applied to PFS by both BICR and investigator. Both results are very close to that from the primary analysis. The median estimates of the BICR PFS with the alternative censoring rule are 13.634 months and NA for senaparib and placebo respectively, and the stratified HR is 0.43 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.58); for investigator PFS, the medians are 11.105 months and NA respectively and the stratified HR is 0.43 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.57).

## 5.3.4. In vitro biomarker test for patient selection for efficacy

No selection on BRCA or HRD status was planned. However, BRCA and HRD status were determined to explore subgroups of interest.

For the central testing of BRCA1/2 mutations, it was based on next-generation sequencing (NGS) using the Illumina platform by AmoyDx. The library preparation kit used is the BRCA1/2 Gene Mutation Detection Kit (HANDLES System) (AmoyDx), the sequencing platform is Illumina MiSeqDx, and the variant analysis software employs AmoyDx's in-house Analysis Software.

The HRD (Homologous Recombination Deficiency) testing was performed by Illumina TSO500 HRD panel in Teddy Clinical Research Laboratory. The HRD detection probe set and algorithm of Illumina TSO500 HRD panel are fully licenced from Myriad Genetics. HRD was defined as per the FDA-approved definition, including a genomic instability score (GIS) of ≥42 or BRCA mutations.

## 5.3.5. Supportive studies

## 5.3.5.1. IMP4297-2018-CN01 (Study 201)

IMP4297-2018-CN01 (hereinafter referred to as Study 201) is a multicentre, open-label, single-arm, non-randomized Phase II clinical study conducted in China. It aims to evaluate the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of senaparib capsules in treating subjects with advanced ovarian cancer carrying germline and/or somatic BRCA1/2 mutations, and to observe the PK characteristics of senaparib.

Study Design

Abbreviations: CA125 = carbohydrate antigen 125 ; CR = complete response; C1D1 = Cycle 1 Day 1; gBRCAmut = germline breast cancer susceptibility gene mutation; PopPK = population pharmacokinetics; PR = partial response; RECIST = Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumours; sBRCAmut = somatic breast cancer susceptibility gene mutation.

Successfully enrolled subjects received senaparib at a dose of 100 mg/dose (QD, under fasting condition, with 28 days as one cycle) until any of the following events occurred: initiation of new antitumour therapy, disease progression, subject withdrawal of ICF, and/or death.

## Study Population

- Confirmed germline and/or somatic BRCA1/2 mutation by central laboratory;
- Previously received ≥2 lines of platinum-containing chemotherapy, with no clinical, CA125, or imaging-confirmed disease recurrence or progression within 6 natural months after completion of the last platinum-based regimen; CA125-evaluated recurrence/progression is defined as: if baseline level exceeds ULN, two consecutive CA125 increases ≥2×baseline; or if baseline is ≤ULN, two consecutive CA125 measurements &gt;2×ULN (Note: ≥7 days between two CA125 tests)

Imaging-confirmed disease recurrence or progression;

## Study Endpoints

Primary endpoint: ORR evaluated by independent imaging based on RECIST v1.1.

Secondary endpoints: ORR, OS, Disease Control Rate (DCR), Clinical Benefit Rate over 6 months (CBR), PFS, and Duration of Response (DOR) evaluated by investigators based on RECIST v1.1.

## Subject Disposition

At the final data cut on 17 December 2024, a total of 93 subjects were enrolled, all of whom received senaparib treatment.

Among the 93 subjects enrolled in this study, the median treatment duration was 288 days. No subjects are still receiving drug therapy. Reasons for discontinuation include PD in 70 subjects (75.3%), AE in 7 subjects (7.5%), subjects' decision in 7 subjects (7.5%), severe protocol noncompliance in 1 subject (1.1%), others in 8 subjects (8.6%).

Demographics and Baseline Characteristics The median age of the 93 subjects enrolled in Study 201 was 55 years, all were female, with a median weight of 62.9 kg, and baseline ECOG performance status scores were all 0 or 1.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

All 93 subjects enrolled in Study 201 carried BRCA1/2 mutations. At initial diagnosis, the clinical stage was mainly Stage III (59.1%), the histological type was mainly serous carcinoma (93.5%), the number of prior lines of standard chemotherapy was mainly second-line (72.0%), and the platinum-free interval was mainly 6-12 months (62.4%) and &gt;12 months (37.6%).

## Efficacy Results

93 patients were enrolled, 92 of whom completed at least one post-baseline tumour evaluation. ORR assessed by IRC was 66.3% (61/92, 95% CI: 55.70%-75.83%), median PFS was 11.14 months (95% CI: 8.28, 13.77), median DOR was 10.35 months (95% CI: 7.49, 12.88) and median OS was 42.45 months.

## 5.3.5.2. Supportive Study: IMP4297-2016-AU01

IMP4297-2016-AU01 (hereinafter referred to as the Study AU01) is an open-label, dose-escalation Phase I study conducted in Australia targeting subjects with advanced solid tumours.

## Study Design

The study included patients with advanced solid tumours who had no standard treatment or had proven ineffective or intolerant to standard treatments, with a preference for patients with advanced breast cancer, ovarian cancer, or prostate cancer

This study was implemented in two phases: the dose-escalation phase aimed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of single and multiple doses of senaparib capsules, and to determine the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) or the recommended Phase II dose (RP2D); the dose-expansion phase aimed to better characterize the safety, tolerability, and PK properties of senaparib, and to further explore the preliminary efficacy of specific doses of senaparib.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

<!-- image -->

## Target Dose:

Starting Dose and DLT Evaluation Window
