| Summary of safety concerns   | Summary of safety concerns   |
|------------------------------|------------------------------|
| Important Identified Risk    | None                         |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

| Important Potential Risks   | Risk 1: Second primary malignancy (other than MDS / AML)   |
|-----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------|
| Missing Information         | None                                                       |

## 6.1.2. Discussion on proposed safety specification

Senaparib has significant haematological toxicity, consistent with non-clinical and clinical findings and the PARP inhibitor class. In the pooled safety dataset, 379/459 subjects (82.6%) experienced haematological toxicity: anaemia 325 (70.8%), leukopenia 284 (61.9%), neutropenia 268 (58.4%), thrombocytopenia 262 (57.1%) and lymphopenia 59 (12.9%). Grade ≥3 events occurred in 234 subjects (51.0%); 205 (44.7%) required dose reduction, 251 (54.7%) dose interruption and 20 (4.4%) permanent discontinuation. These reactions are adequately addressed in SmPC sections 4.2, 4.4 and 4.8. As the risk is well characterised and manageable through routine pharmacovigilance and risk-minimisation measures, deletion of \"haematological toxicity\" from the important identified risks is endorsed. The applicant confirmed continued monitoring through routine pharmacovigilance and PSURs.

Overall, 7 (1.6%) participants reported myelodysplastic syndrome / acute myeloid leukaemia (MDS / AML) among 447 tumour patients who received ≤100 mg senaparib up to 31 May 2025. These effects are listed as common in section 4.8 and covered by a warning in section 4.4. As MDS/AML are known PARP inhibitor risks, it is considered an identified rather than a potential risk and therefore routine risk minimisation measures are sufficient to monitor MDS/AML. The applicant agreed to add MDS/AML as an important identified risk in future PSURs for further characterisation.

Second primary malignancy (other than MDS / AML) is considered as an important potential risk of senaparib related to the interference of PARP inhibition with the ability of cells to repair DNA single strand breaks. This elevated genomic instability could lead to an increased risk of developing second primary tumours. No case of second primary malignancy other than MDS / AML occurred in clinical study.

The applicant's proposal to perform cumulative analyses of cases of \"Second primary malignancy other than MDS / AML\" in the PSURs is supported. Nevertheless, given the seriousness of this potential risk and its possible impact on the benefit-risk balance of senaparib, the collection of safety data should be as comprehensive and detailed as possible to allow an appropriate clinical evaluation with causality assessment.

Regarding Embryofoetal toxicity, senaparib is a PARP inhibitor with strong inhibitory effect on PARP-1 and PARP-2 enzyme activities. The PARP inhibitors are a class of compounds with genotoxicity that can induce active division of target cells (such as bone marrow cells) in animals and humans, thus possessing teratogenicity and/or causing embryo-foetal death. Animal studies involving PARP1/PARP2 inhibitors, which have the same mechanism of actions, have demonstrated embryolethal and teratogenic effects. No pregnancy events were reported in the clinical studies. and no dedicated reproductive and developmental toxicity study was conducted, but embryo-foetal toxicity is strongly expected based on the mechanism of action. The product information adequately reflects this risk, which should continue to be monitored through PSUR reporting and assessment. Embryofoetal toxicity has been deleted from the safety concerns of the RMP.

## 8.1.1. SmPC section 4.1 justification

The approved indication is supported by the results of the pivotal clinical study submitted in the dossier, a phase 3 randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled study conducted in China to evaluate the efficacy and safety of senaparib maintenance monotherapy following first-line platinum-based chemotherapy in patients with FIGO stage III-IV ovarian cancer. The proposed indication is for \"allcomers\" population, regardless any biomarker status. Pursuant to Article 23(1) of Regulation No (EU) 726/2004, Sepalna (senaparib) is included in the additional monitoring list since it contains a new active substance which, on 1 January 2011, was not contained in any medicinal product authorised in the EU.

Therefore, the summary of product characteristics and the package leaflet include a statement that this medicinal product is subject to additional monitoring and will allow quick identification of new safety information. The statement is preceded by an inverted equilateral black triangle.

## 9. Benefit-risk assessment

## 9.1. Therapeutic context

The agreed indication is:

Sepalna is indicated as monotherapy for the maintenance treatment of adult patients with advanced (FIGO Stages III and IV) epithelial high-grade ovarian, fallopian tube or primary peritoneal cancer who are in response (complete or partial) following completion of first-line platinum-based chemotherapy.

## 9.1.1. Disease or condition, proposed therapeutic indication

EOC is typically characterised by a progressive and often asymptomatic course. Because early disease is largely silent, more than two-thirds of women are diagnosed at an advanced FIGO stage (III-IV), when peritoneal dissemination and ascites are already present (Renz et al., Obstetrics and Gynecology, 2025; Ledermann et al .Ann Oncol 2024). The disease tends to spread within the peritoneal cavity rather than through hematogenous routes. Symptoms are usually nonspecific, leading to diagnostic delays. Common features include abdominal bloating, distension, pelvic or abdominal pain, early satiety, urinary frequency, and fatigue. In advanced disease, ascites, pleural effusions, weight loss, and bowel obstruction may occur (González-Martín A, et al. Ann Oncol, 2023, Goff BA et al. Cancer 2007).

The cumulative risk of ovarian cancer to age 80 years has been estimated at 44% for BRCA1 and 17% for BRCA2 pathogenic variant carriers (Kuchenbaecker et al., JAMA 2017). Around 13-15% of ovarian cancers are attributable to heritable BRCA1/2 mutations, and additional cases carry somatic BRCA alterations (Konstantinopoulos PA et al. J Clin Oncol 2020).

## 9.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

First-line treatment of newly diagnosed ovarian cancer combines maximal cytoreductive surgery and platinum-based chemotherapy. Bevacizumab can be added concurrently and continued as maintenance.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Maintenance therapy after response to first-line (Stage III/IV) is recommended and is stratified according to BRCA status. Patients who are BRCA-mutated are recommended to receive PARPis (olaparib, niraparib or rucaparib). Bevacizumab can also be added to olaparib in this setting.

Patients who are BRCA-wild-type and HRD-positive receive olaparib + bevacizumab (if bevacizumab in induction), niraparib monotherapy or rucaparib monotherapy.

Patients with a HRD-negative (HRP) status are recommended to receive bevacizumab maintenance (when started with chemo). PARP inhibitors yield modest and heterogeneous PFS gains without OS benefit to date (Gonzalez-Martin et al. 2026, Renz et al., 2025, Vergote et al. 2021).

Nevertheless, up to 70% of patients with stage III-IV high-grade ovarian cancer will relapse within 3 years, therefore, an unmet medical need remains in newly diagnosed EOC (Gonzalez-Martin et al. 2023).

For a detailed description, please see section 2.1. of this document.

## 9.2. Main clinical studies

The pivotal study supporting the sought indication is a randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicentre Phase III clinical study conducted in China, aiming to evaluate the efficacy and safety of senaparib monotherapy maintenance treatment in subjects with FIGO stage III-IV ovarian cancer after first-line platinum-based chemotherapy.

This study randomised 404 patients with newly diagnosed FIGO stage III-IV high grade serous (HGS), high grade endometrioid (HGE) or other histological types of ovarian cancer, fallopian tube cancer or primary peritoneal cancer who have had a complete (CR) or partial response (PR) after first-line platinum-based chemotherapy. Patients were randomly assigned to senaparib or placebo at a 2:1 ratio. Randomisation was performed within 8 weeks after the last dose of chemotherapy (i.e., the day of the last infusion).

Subjects enrolled in the study received senaparib or matching placebo at the dose of 100 mg once daily (quaque die, QD) orally on an empty stomach, in 28-day cycles. The study treatment was planned to be administered for 2 years or until PD, whichever occurs earlier.

For a detailed description of the main clinical studies supporting this application, please refer to section 5.3.2. of this document.

## 9.3. Favourable effects

At the DCO of 16 March 2023, the primary endpoint PFS by BICR was met in the ITT population with a HR of 0.43 (95% CI: 0.32, 0.58, p&lt;0.0001) in favour of the senaparib arm, mPFS (95% CI) was 13.63 months in the placebo arm (10.86, 16.62) and not reached in senaparib arm. The median follow-up durations for the senaparib group and the placebo group were 22.37 months (0.1-38.2 months) and 22.18 months (4.2-35.4 months), respectively.

Subgroup analyses were consistent with the primary analysis. In particular, the HRs for the subgroups with baseline BRCA mutation and BRCA wildtype status were 0.43 (95% CI: 0.24, 0.76) and 0.43 (95% CI: 0.30, 0.61), with P values of 0.0026 and &lt;0.0001, respectively.

At the interim analysis for OS (around 100 OS events, DCO 17 May 2024), mOS were still not reached in both treatment arms but no detrimental effect was observed on OS.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 9.3.1. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

The OS data immaturity at both conducted analyses does not allow to assess the magnitude of the effect on OS, although data do not suggest a potential detrimental effect. Further data will be obtained from the submission of the final overall survival results (including by subgroups based on BRCA1/2 mutations and HRD biomarkers) of study IMP4297-301 FLAMES, a phase III randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter study (see Annex II).

## 9.4. Unfavourable effects

The safety data for senaparib was derived primarily from an ongoing pivotal randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicentre phase III clinical study that included 403 subjects, of whom270 received senaparib and 133 received placebo. Additional safety data were provided from three supportive studies, one drug-drug interaction study and one phase Ib/II study. Overall, 459 participants received at least one dose of senaparib.

The safety profile of senaparib was mainly driven by haematological toxicity including anaemia (81.9% vs 18.8% in placebo), neutrophil count decreased (76.3% vs 31.6%), white blood cell count decreased (75.2% vs 28.6%), and platelet count decreased (70.0% vs 15.8%). In the pooled analysis, the median time to onset of haematological toxicity was 17 days (2-784 days), and the median duration 22.5 days (2.5-648 days). Overall, these ADRs were manageable with dose reduction or interruption.

The other most frequently reported PT in the pivotal study were transaminases increased (55.6% vs 42.1%), nausea (28.1% vs 9.8%) and asthenia (21.5% vs 8.3%).

Regarding serious TEAE, in the pivotal study, the most frequent PT were anaemia (26 [9.6%]), platelet count decreased (21 [7.8%] and intestinal obstruction (7 [2.6%]).

The incidence of death in senaparib group was lower than that of placebo group (61 [22.6%] vs 39 [29.3%]). The main cause of death was disease progression in both groups (36 [59.0%] vs. 20 [51.3%]). In the supportive pooled analysis, 5 (1.1%) additional subjects experienced fatal TEAEs: one related bone marrow failure as well as cerebral haemorrhage, cerebral infarction, intestinal obstruction and haemorrhagic shock.

Finally, based on the available data, 'Second primary malignancy (other than MDS/AML)' has been identified as an important potential risk in the RMP for senaparib.

## 9.4.1. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

The pivotal trial enrolled only Chinese patients. Supportive studies in patients with solid tumours included comparative safety data from 59 Asian and 35 White patients. Although the sample sizes were limited, the available data did not indicate clinically meaningful differences in the safety profile between Asian and White patients. The apparent lower incidence of haematological toxicity observed in White patients was not supported by the corresponding laboratory findings. Overall, the safety profile appears comparable between the two groups while acknowledging the limited size of the supportive dataset.

Senaparib has the potential to affect several drug-metabolising enzymes and to affect hepatic transporters. To further characterise its drug-drug interaction potential, the Applicant will conduct a clinical study with midazolam (REC) to determine the effect of senaparib on CYP3A4 substrates. This study will include measurement of plasma coproporphyrin I as an endogenous biomarker of OATP1B activity. In addition, a clinical cocktail study will be conducted to assess the effect of senaparib or the active metabolite M9, on substrates of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19. These studies are included as recommendations (REC).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 9.6. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 9.6.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

The results to support the benefit-risk of senaparib as monotherapy maintenance treatment are coming from a randomised clinical trial in a clinical setting where other medicinal products of the same pharmacological class showed benefit. In the ITT population, the statistically significant improvement in mPFS, assessed by BICR, in senaparib arm compared to placebo arm is also considered clinically relevant with at least a 11 months difference between both treatment arms, although the mPFS in senaparib arm was not reached yet at the DCO of the primary analysis. Preplanned subgroup analyses were also consistent with the primary analysis. In particular, mPFS according to BRCA mutation status showed similar HR for BRCA mutated and wildtype than the primary analysis.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Overall, results in the ITT are statistically significant, of clinical relevance and consistent with the previously authorised PARPi.

At the time of the interim OS analysis there was no indication of detrimental effect, but the OS data remain immature. In order to further characterise the efficacy of senaparib, the applicant will submit the final overall survival results (including by subgroups based on BRCA1/2 mutations and HRD biomarkers) of study IMP4297-301 FLAMES as a PAES in Annex II.D by 30 June 2027.

The safety profile of senaparib is primarily related to hematologic toxicity (anaemia, neutrophil count decreased, white blood cell count decreased and platelet count decreased), reported in more than 80% of participants in the pivotal study and pooled study. The majority of the events were related to senaparib with a median time to onset of 17 days, and a median duration of 22.5 days. The observed haematological toxicity markedly affected tolerability as dose adjustments were needed in the majority of patients (73% of the patients experienced a dose interruption and 64% of patients had at least one dose reduction). Overall, 5.2% of patients permanently discontinued treatment. The other most frequently reported PT were transaminases increased, nausea and asthenia. The potentially lifethreatening adverse events of MDS/AML, known for PARP inhibitors, appear to accumulate with longer follow-up. While during the trial reporting period, 3 patients (1.1%) in the senaparib arm of study 301 reported MDS/AML, no patients in the placebo group reported such cases, and during the additional follow up, 3 more cases of MDS/AML in the senaparib arm of study 301 (2.2%) and 1 case in study IMP4297-2018-CN01 were reported. All patients had potential contributing factors for the development of MDS/AML.

Overall, the pattern of reported adverse events is in line with the reported common adverse events for other PARP inhibitors.

## 9.6.2. Balance of benefits and risks

The benefit of senaparib as a maintenance treatment in the overall population of patients with advanced epithelial ovarian cancer who achieve a response after first chemotherapy are overall statistically significant and considered clinically relevant. Although OS data remain immature, the interim OS analysis showed no evidence of a detrimental effect.

The safety profile of senaparib is primarily related to hematologic toxicity and ADRs were overall manageable with dose reduction or interruption.

## 9.6.2.1. Third party intervention(s)

Comments were received from healthcare professional organisation in the context of CHMP early dialogue, stating that any maintenance treatment able to delay recurrence or improvement on existing chemotherapy results is valuable, but its toxicity should be weighed against potential overall survival benefits, not just the postponement of recurrence. Overall survival remains the most important endpoint and should be measurable as a co-primary or key secondary endpoint without unduly delaying the readout of primary results.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 9.7. Benefit-risk conclusions

The benefit-risk balance of Sepalna is considered positive.

The magnitude of OS benefit, however, remains uncertain due to the data immaturity. Therefore, in order to characterise the efficacy of senaparib as monotherapy for the maintenance treatment of advanced epithelial high-grade ovarian, fallopian tube or primary peritoneal cancer in adult patients who are in response following completion of first-line platinum-based chemotherapy, the applicant has committed to submit the final overall survival results (including by subgroups based on BRCA1/2 mutations and HRD biomarkers) of study IMP4297-301 FLAMES as a PAES in Annex II.D by 30 June 2027.
